115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

短評 
⚫ 美國經濟目前仍維持溫和擴張，內需與投資表現優於預期，短期衰退風
險有限 ； 惟近期新增就業明顯低於預期 ， 企業聘僱意願與職缺需求放緩 ，
勞動市場動能已有轉弱跡象，而通膨受能源價格、 AI 投資及關稅等因
素影響仍具黏著性。在經濟成長放緩與通膨尚未完全受控的環境下，聯
準會短期可能傾向維持觀望，惟若通膨再度升溫，不排除年底前仍有進
一步升息空間。

事件

短評

因素，可能性相對較低。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.5 
一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

### Page 6

用相關內容。

5

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

用相關內容。

6

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

環保法規，須負擔高昂翻修成本，使法國房價呈現連三季下滑所拖累。 
 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長 
(1)新加坡：受服務業、零售暨其他商品業，以及食品價格漲幅擴大影響，8 月消
費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.3%、不包括住宿及個人交通費用之核心 CPI 年
增率升至 2.2%， 連續3 個月上升並創 2 年新高 。 受全球能源價格居高不下及氣候
條件不佳影響，預期新加坡進口食品、商品及服務成本將持續上升，惟國內生產
率成長及政府補貼政策將抑制部分服務業通膨，新加坡金管局(MAS)或在 10 月
「略微」緊縮貨幣政策。(2)泰國：8 月出口額年增 24.3%(346 億美元)，主要由工
業產品出口成長 27.1%所帶動，其中電腦及相關設備（年增 42.1%）、電話及零
件（151.2%） 及電路板 （19.2%） 增幅最為顯著，對美國出口年增48.7%、對中國
和日本分別成長 15.5%、19.6%。8 月進口額年增 25.1%(371 億美元)，以燃料 （年
增 42.8%） 、 資本貨物 （33.9%） 、 其他貨物 （27.4%） 、 原材料和中間產品 （21.3%）、
運輸設備（13.6%）為主。進出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差 24.8 億美元，較 7 月略收
窄，為連續第 11 個月出現逆差。(3)菲律賓：8 月出口額大增 27.8%(91 億美元)，
創兩年新高，主要得益於電子產品（年增 59.9%） ，尤其是半導體（73.5%）及黃

六、台灣—景氣對策信號連九月呈紅燈

2026 年 10 月 9 日 全9 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 8 月消費統計 
耐久財とサービスが弱く、総じて見れば前月から減少

経済調査部 エコノミスト 龐 鈞文

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年8 月の家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比＋0.1％と2 カ月
連続で増加した。 一方、複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（ CTI
ミクロ）で見た実質消費は同▲2.3％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。また、供給側統計の 1
つである商業動態統計では、CPI の財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。
総じて見れば、8 月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。 
◼ 先行きの個人消費は 底堅く推移するとみている 。名目賃金は春闘での高水準の賃上げ
や最低賃金の引き上げもあって上昇が続く見込みである。 一方、政府の物価高対策 の
効果の剝落などが物価を押し上げる要因となろう。加えて、原油の輸入価格は代替調
達もあって高止まりしており、幅広い分野の価格への波及を通じて個人消費を下押し
する可能性には引き続き注意が必要だ。

＜2026 年8 月の消費総括＞耐久財やサービスを中心に減少 
需要側統計である家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比＋0.1％と2カ月
連続で小幅に増加した一方、複数の需要側統計を用いて補正した世帯消費動向指数（CTI ミク
ロ）で見た実質消費は同▲2.3％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した（図表 1）。また、供給側統計の 1 つ
である商業動態統計では、CPI の財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。総じて見
れば、2026年8月の個人消費は前月から減少したと判断される。

＜CTI ミクロ・家計調査（需要側）＞「交通・通信」などの7 費目が減少 
2026年 8 月の CTIミクロ（二人以上の世帯）を費目別に見ると、10 大費目1のうち、 「交通・
通信」 （前月比▲5.3％） 、「その他」 （同▲4.1％）、 「教養娯楽」 （同▲2.9％） 、 「教育」 （同▲
4.7％） 、「家具・家事用品」 （同▲4.4％） 、「被服及び履物」 （同▲3.7％） 、「保健医療」 （同▲
0.5％）の7費目が減少した。 
他方、「光熱・水道」 （前月比＋4.4％） 、「住居」 （同＋3.3％） 、「食料」 （同＋0.5％）の3費目
が増加した（図表2）。

続いて、CTIミクロの10大費目に含まれる個別品目への支出について確認する。 
CTI ミクロにおける「交通・通信」は 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。自動車等関係費などへの支出
が減少した。「その他」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。諸雑費やこづかいなどへの支出が減少した。
「教養娯楽」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。教養娯楽サービスを中心に、教養娯楽用品などへの支出
が減少した。 「教育」は2 カ月連続で減少した。授業料等を中心に支出が減少した2。「家具・家
事用品」は2カ月ぶりに減少した。家庭用耐久財を中心に支出が減少した。「被服及び履物」は
2 カ月ぶりに減少した。洋服を中心に支出が減少した。「保健医療」は 2 カ月連続で減少した。
保健医療サービスを中心に支出が減少した。 
他方、「光熱・水道」は 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。電気代やガス代を中心に支出が拡大した。
「住居」は 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。家賃地代を中心に支出が拡大した。「食料」は 2 カ月連続で
増加した。酒類や野菜・海藻を中心に支出が拡大した。 
家計調査における二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は、前月比＋0.1％と2カ月連続で小幅に増加
した。ただし、振れの大きい項目を除いた、住居等を除くベースでは同▲1.7％と2カ月ぶりに
減少した（図表 3 上段左）。 
基礎的支出は前月比＋0.9％と、4 カ月ぶりに増加した。また、選択的支出は同＋1.3％と、2
カ月連続で増加した（いずれも大和総研による季節調整値、図表3 中段左）。 
消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると（大和総研による季節調整値、図表 3 中段右）、耐久財
（前月比＋8.2％）が4カ月ぶりに増加し、全体を押し上げた。非耐久財（同＋0.4％）も3カ月
ぶりに増加した。一方、サービス（同▲0.3％）と半耐久財（同▲0.1％）は減少した。

2 ただし、授業料等は児童や学生がいる一部の世帯以外はほとんど支出しないため、サンプルサイズが小さく
振れが大きい。

＜商業動態統計（供給側）＞小売販売額は名目・実質ともに2 カ月ぶりに減少 
2026 年 8 月の商業動態統計によると、名目小売販売額は前月比▲1.2％と 2 カ月ぶりに減少
した（図表 4、5） 。また、CPIの財指数で実質化した小売販売額は同▲1.1％だった。 
名目小売販売額を業種別に見ると、全ての業種が前月から減少した。「機械器具小売業」 （前
月比▲6.9％）は 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。「自動車小売業」 （同▲2.5％）は 4 カ月連続で減少し
た。8 月の新車販売台数（大和総研による季節調整値）は同▲7.6％と減少しており3、この結
果とも整合的だ。「燃料小売業」 （同▲1.8％）は2カ月ぶりに減少した。「飲食料品小売業」 （同
▲0.4％）は2カ月ぶりに減少した。コンビニの加工食品（同▲1.5％）が減少した。「織物・衣
服・身の回り品小売業」 （同▲1.8％）、「その他小売業」 （同▲0.2％）、「各種商品小売業」 （同
▲0.4％）はいずれも2カ月ぶりに減少した。

7 / 9

＜2026 年9 月の消費＞8 月から減少した可能性 
業界統計や個社データ、JCB 消費 NOW をもとに判断すると、2026 年 9 月の消費は 8 月から減
少したとみている4。財消費では、9 月前半の実績をもとに試算したスーパーの JCB 消費額（大
和総研による季節調整値）と家電の JCB 消費額（同）は減少した。一方、アパレルの売上高は
堅調に推移し、新車販売台数（同）も増加した。サービス消費では、宿泊の JCB 消費額（同）
と外食の JCB 消費額（同）は天候不順もあっていずれも前月から減少した。一方、新幹線輸送
量の前年比伸び率は前月から拡大した。

＜先行き＞個人消費は底堅く推移する見込み／価格上昇の広がりに注意 
先行きの個人消費は底堅く推移するとみている。名目賃金の伸びが高水準で推移する一方、
政府の物価高対策による物価の押し下げ効果の剝落などで、物価上昇率が高まり、実質賃金が
伸び悩む可能性がある。 
名目賃金の伸びは高水準で推移するだろう。春闘の結果5は前年をやや下回るものの、高水準
となった。また、 2026 年度の最低賃金の 引き上げ率は全国加重平均で＋ 5.0％（前年度は＋
6.3％）と高水準を維持した6。 
消費者物価について、7 月使用分から 9 月使用分まで実施される電気・ガス料金の補助とい
った政府の物価高対策が終了した。加えて、代替調達もあって原油の輸入価格は高止まりして
おり7、原材料費や輸送費の上昇を通じて幅広い分野の価格に波及し、消費を下押しする可能性
には注意が必要だ。帝国データバンクによると、値上げされた飲食料品のうち中東情勢の影響
を受けた品目の割合は 9 月末時点で約 3 割だった。今後も値上げが継続する見込みだが、値上
げの品目数は10月で一旦ピークアウトする可能性もある8。 
また、金利上昇による住宅ローン等の利払い負担増が個人消費にもたらす悪影響にも警戒す
る必要がある9。さらに、消費動向調査（内閣府）によると、9 月の消費者態度指数（二人以上
の世帯、季節調整値 ）は 35.4（前月差▲0.1pt）と小幅に低下し、依然として低水準にあり
（巻末図表「消費者マインド」参照） 、節約志向の高まりに注意が必要だ。

4 詳細は、拙稿「消費データブック（2026/10/6 号）」（大和総研レポート、2026 年10月 6 日）を参照。 
5 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3 年連続の 5％超え！～2026 春季生活闘争 第7 回（最終）回答集計結果
について～」（2026 年7 月3日）によると、定昇相当込みの賃上げ率の平均は 5.01％（前年同時期は
5.25％）となった。 
6 厚生労働省「地域別最低賃金の全国一覧」（2026 年10 月9 日閲覧） 
7 世界的な需給の引き締まりも受け、ホルムズ海峡を経由しないルートでの中東や他地域からの原油の代替調
達の価格が割高となり、調達コストが押し上げられている。詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田
凱「日本経済見通し：2026 年8 月」（大和総研レポート、2026 年8 月 24 日）を参照。 
8 帝国データバンク「『食品主要 195 社』価格改定動向調査 ― 2026 年 10 月 飲食料品値上げ、10 月は3153
品目 値上げラッシュはピーク越え」（2026 年9 月30 日） 
9 詳細は、神田慶司・畑中宏仁・中村華奈子・横田凱「日本経済見通し：2026 年8 月」（大和総研レポート、
2026 年8 月24 日）を参照。

AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
September Sales Rose 3%
MoM, 27% YoY
Here we focus on Airtac's monthly sales, which we believe could
be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• September sales came in at NT$3,811mn (+3% MoM/+27% YoY).
• 3Q26 revenue was NT$11,512mn (-5% QoQ/+40% YoY), in line with our
estimate of NT$11,586mn (-5% QoQ/+41% YoY) but slightly better than
consensus at NT$11,215mn (-8% QoQ/+36% YoY).
• By segment, shipments of machine tool (+35% YoY), energy & lighting (+30%

YoY), electronics (+27% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY) outperformed during
the month.
Outlook:
• Both order booking and shipments continued to exceed company
expectations in the month.
• Looking ahead, management remains positive on its growth outlook, driven
by share gains and stimulus policies, which could help sustain the upcycle at
least into 2027.
• We remain OW as current valuation at 18x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs. its
mid-cycle average of 25x, though we think Hiwin (2049.TW) might

outperform on share price on the back of sequential growth in the top line
and potentially GM in 3Q26.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Alchip Technologies
Better allocation into 2027E; N2 project on track
Q3 sales up 243% QoQ on Trainium 3 ramp
Alchip reported September sales of NT$9,979 mn, up 14% MoM and 496% YoY,
concluding Q326 sales of NT$26,188 mn for +243% QoQ and 299% YoY, slightly
ahead of consensus, although below UBSe as we assumed a higher wafer allocation for
the Trainium 3 project, while sales contribution for its Chinese automotive customer and

NRE might also have came in lower than our expectation. We now expect Alchip's Q326
GM to arrive at 20.1%, down from 34.8% in Q226 on less favourable mix, leading to
OPM of 15.9%. We forecast Q326 EPS to grow 132% QoQ to NT$46.34.
Trainium 3  to sustain longer with potential upside; Trainium 4 tape-out on track
We expect Alchip's Trainium 3 monthly shipment run-rate to retain at current level into
H127F, and could extend into H227F with potential better allocation for Alchip with a

similar margin profile, subject to the final negotiation with its customer. We think
Trainium 3 deployments could stretch a little bit longer with a limited gap (vs prior ~3
months) before the next-generation Trainium 4 launch given the 3nm capacity tightness.
We continue to forecast overall Trainium 3 shipments at ~1.8mn units for 2026 and will
increase to ~2.8 mn units in 2027E, up 55% YoY, although Alchip's Trainium 3 shipment
could outgrow at 80%+ YoY given potential better allocation. For the Trainium 4 project

(2nm node), management noted it remains on track for tape-out before year-end and
still targets mass production by late-2027 or early 2028. Alchip continues to believe it
could enjoy a higher value-add given the increasing design complexity on chiplet and
higher wafer pricing for the 2nm node, while GM could retain at a similar level
compared with the current N3 project.
Engaging new customers for diversification
Alchip noted it remains in talks with another major CSP customer for potential adoption

of COT (customer-owned tooling) business model in the future for further cost saving.
We believe Alchip's competitive pricing,  strength for faster turnaround, and advanced
packaging knowledge put Alchip in a  good position to capture this opportunity, if the
customer decides to change the business model. Nevertheless, we do not expect
meaningful revenue contribution in 2027-28E from the new CSP customer. On the other
hand, we believe Alchip is in talks with other HPC customers for mid-size physical design

projects and this is likely to become a new revenue stream from 2027E. We estimate it
could book US$200-300mn revenue in 2027E from the new HPC customer.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with NT$6,600 price target
We lower our 2026E EPS by 11% primarily on lower Trainium 3 and automotive
contribution. We raise 2027E/28E EPS by 2%/5% to reflect  revenue contribution from
new HPC customer, offset by higher opex. We keep our PT unchanged at NT$6,600,
based on the same 30x 2027E EPS (vs. a 15-50x PE range). Stock is trading at ~20x 2027

P/E, below its peers and past five year average of 30x.

Company Description
Alchip was founded in 2002 by a group of Cadence managers with 20+ years of experience in
the semiconductor industry. It is an IC design and production service provider, and
management believes it can leverage third-party IP to enable customized chipset solutions for

customers cost effectively and fast to market. The company was funded by Cisco Investment
and Softbank Investment with VC funding in early 2000, then had its IPO on the emerging
board in Taiwan in 2010 and the main board in 2014.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Alchip using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced node project diversification and
ramp, and growing competition in advanced projects from other IC design service companies.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

### Page 1

M
Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.

TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Reﬂecting robust AI capex guidance from

global CSPs, we are OW on TSMC.
▪ Our estimates reﬂect TSMC’s strong
proﬁtability and another year of >40% Y/Y
revenue growth seen for 2026.
▪ Leading-edge (7nm below) demand
remains robust.
▪ Reﬂecting increased bargaining power,
long-term margin expansion, and sustainable
AI semi demand, we expect the stock to re-
rate to our implied 2027e target P/E of 20x.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Quanta (2382.TW): AI servers ramp up to drive growth ahead; model 
transition and PC shipment slowdown could weigh on 4Q26; Neutral
19 October 2026 | 1:57AM HKT
Quanta Sep revenues declined by 23% MoM, which we attributed to AI servers model 
transition, while PC entering quarter-end at strong MoM of +62% MoM. Looking 
forward, we expect monthly revenues to resume sequential growth in Oct to Dec, 
riding on reducing impact of AI servers model transition as new racks ramping up, 
along with growing general servers on agentic AI, and ASP increase on better 
specification and high raw material costs. We expect 4Q26E revenues to decline by 
4% QoQ, considering the high base in 3Q26 and the gradual ramp up of new AI 
server racks. As we highlighted in Oct (Global Server TAM updates), we expect global 
AI server racks (powered by NVIDIA and AMD) shipment at 63k / 110k / 164k in 
2026E-28E along with the specification upgrade to drive the ASP; we raised leading 
US CSPs in aggregate capex by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026E-28E to US$1,397bn by 
2028E, reflecting our positive view on AI spending. Maintain Neutral on fair 
valuation.

Earnings revision: We raise revenues by 1% / 23% / 30% in 2026E-28E to reflect the 
AI server racks shipment ramp up and higher ASP on better specification. Given the

### Page 2

much lower GM of AI server racks compared to Quanta’s other products, our changes in 
gross profits narrow to 2% / 5% / 5% in 2026E-28E. Our opex ratio remains largely 
unchanged in 2026E-27E, while further improves in 2028E reflecting the enhancing 
operation efficiency when revenues scale increases and harvest from previous R&D 
investments. We change our non-op income and tax rate estimates in 2026E-28E closer 
to historical trend. Our net income is thus changed by -2% / +0% / +14% in 2026E-28E.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12M TP . Our target P/E 
multiple is at 10.7x 2027E (no change), which is derived from the refreshed peers’ 
correlation between forward trading P/E and earnings growth and Quanta’s forward year 
earnings growth. With our updated earnings estimates our 12M TP is revised up to 
NT$300 (vs. NT$299 previously). Our target P/E is still between the company’s historical 
avg. and avg. -1stv. trading P/E. Maintain Neutral.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$300 is based on an 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. Key 
upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

its gas imports

from the Gulf region. However, a few factors have attenuated the shock  
resulting from the Iran war
 , as  
V
ietnam  
has reacted swiftly, finding alternative suppliers, introducing measures to contain demand and using its (low) stocks. 
 The country 
produces 70% of its needs for refined products and imports the remainder from Asian countries  
(
though the region’s production is 
dependent on imports from the Gulf countries
 )
. More positively, oil 
 and 
gas account for only 35% of 
 Vietnam
 ’s energy mix (coal: 54% 
and 
renewables: 11%), and the country can part
 ial
 ly switch to coal for electricity generation.  
◼ 
 The potential pass
 -
 through of rising global energy prices to domestic inflation is high. Yet, the government has some fiscal policy 
leeway 
 (
given its moderate deficit and debt ratios
 ) 
and has introduced small measures to contain inflation (
 such as the 
 use of 
 the 
price stabili
 s
ation fund  
and 
 fuel
-
 tax suspension until 
 the 
end 
of 
June). CPI inflation 
 rose to 
4.
4
% 
y/y in 
 the first eight months of 
 2026, 
up from 
 3.
3
% in 
 2025
. 
It is projected to 
 average  
4.
4
% in 2026 
 – 
which is still within  
the 
target 
set by the central bank (S
 B
V) 
for this 
year
, 
but also the highest rate since 2013. This will dampen consumers’ purchasing power. The SBV is projected to keep policy rates  
stable in the 
 short term
 , unless inflation accelerates more than expected or depreciation pressures on the dong intensify too much.

VIETNAM HAS NOT YET FIXED ITS EXTERNAL LIQUIDITY PROBLEM
, WHICH IS A SOURCE OF VULNERABILITY 
◼ 
 V
ietnam
 ’s trade balance 
 has 
 deteriorated 
 steadily 
 in 2026. It turned in
 to a deficit  
amounting to 
 USD
19 
bn 
in the first eight months of 
2026 
 (customs data)
 , 
 compared to  
 a surplus 
 in the previous year 
 (
amounting to 
 USD
14 
 bn 
 in the first eight months of 2025
 ).  
This 
 rapid deterioration 
 has resulted notably from the ris
 ing 
import prices of both energy and electronic inputs.  
◼ 
 On the one hand, the current account surplus 
 is 
 decreas
 ing, bu
 t from a comfortable level
 . 
It is projected to narrow 
 to 
2.9
% of GDP in 
2026 from 6.7% in 2025, due to  
the larger energy trade deficit (
 which 
 accounted for  
3.6% of GDP in 2025), the larger trade deficit in 
semiconductors/tech goods, and lower export receipts from tourism 
 (about 
 6% of GDP). 
 Furthermore
 , net FDI  
 inflows will remain 
strong,  
staying close to 4% of GDP in 2026
 . 
Therefore, 
 Vietnam is  
unlikely to need to incur new foreign debt.  
◼ 
 On the other hand, V
 ietnam
 ’s forex reserves are low and do not provide a solid cushion  
 against a deterioration 
 in 
 the balance of 
payments
 . Forex reserves amounted to USD8
 8
.
3 
bn at the end of 
 June 2026
 , which is 
 weak: it is 
 enough 
to service external debt in 
the 
 short ter
 m 
 but covers less than 3 months of imports of goods 
 and 
 services. In 
 particular, i
 f 
 resident capital outflows increase 
(residents’ confidence in the dong 
 may 
be eroded by ri
 s
in
g 
inflation and the external energy shock), currency pressure could intensify 
and the central bank could have difficulties to defend the dong. 
 This risk cannot be ignored
 , 
 even 
 though 
 t
he VND has barely 
depreciated 
 this year (
 the 
central 
 reference 
rate has lost a small 2% against the USD since  
the beginning of 2026
 )
.

MEDIUM
-
TERM PROSPECTS ARE FAVO
U
RABLE 
◼ 
 In the medium term, economic growth is expected to remain strong. It is projected at 5.9% per year on average in 2027
 –
2031, 
compared to  
6.2% in the past decade. This is one of the highest growth rates expected in the ASEAN region.  
◼ 
 This  
projection is based on a rather optimistic scenario, as it assumes that:  
i
) FDI continues to flow in, and the manufacturing export sector expands further
 . This 
 means Vietnam’s external accounts 
 continue 
to 
strengthen in the medium term, thanks to continued 
 current account surpluses
 , 
lower resident capital outflows, declining external 
debt and slowly increasing forex reserves
 ; 
ii
) 
D
emographic trends remain 
 favourable
 , even 
 though they 
 are projected to 
 weaken gradually  
in the medium
 -
 long term
 ;  
iii
) 
 Structural r
 eforms make steady progress, helping to improve financial
 -
sector supervision, ease infrastructure constraints in the 
energy and water sectors, boost aggregate productivity (
 i.e. 
 reducing the gap between high
 -
 productivity FDI firms and low
 -
productivity local sectors) and adapt to climate change.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

Document 282: CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng Không Taseco - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-22).pdf

### Page 1

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trưởng tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trưởng khách qua cảng hàng 
không thúc đẩy doanh thu tại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nhận doanh thu và LNST đạt 479/92 tỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng chờ thương gia. Lũy kế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đạt 1,035/221 tỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác 
tăng bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế 
gia tăng được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 
182 điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp từ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất và 6 điểm mới tại 
T2 Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Quốc và Gia Bình tiếp tục mở 
ra dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh được kỳ vọng 
tác động tích cực lên biên lợi nhuận của AST. Chúng tôi dự phóng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp đạt 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 
48%/17% yoy.

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với AST, giá mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco là một 
trong những doanh nghiệp có quy mô mạng 
lưới điểm kinh doanh lớn trong lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ phi hàng không tại Việt Nam, với 141 
điểm kinh doanh tại 6 sân bay quốc tế tính 
đến cuối tháng 6/2026. Hoạt động cốt lõi của 
công ty bao gồm bán lẻ bách hóa lưu niệm 
(BHLN), hàng miễn thuế, dịch vụ ăn uống và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Ngoài ra, AST còn 
tham gia lĩnh vực suất ăn hàng không thông 
qua khoản đầu tư tại công ty liên kết VinaCS. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Doanh thu trên các điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu tăng nhờ lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa dự phóng tăng 
11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng trưởng mới, với số điểm kinh doanh AST 2026 dự kiến đạt 145 
điểm và tăng lên 182 điểm vào 2030. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Biên lợi nhuận gộp cải thiện lên 64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027 nhờ mở rộng hàng miễn thuế, 
phòng chờ và tối ưu vận hành.

điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng tăng trưởng lưu lượng hành khách, đặc biệt là sự gia tăng tỷ 
trọng khách quốc tế, sẽ thúc đẩy doanh thu và hiệu quả kinh doanh của AST 
trong giai đoạn 2026–2030. Theo Cục Hàng không Việt Nam, lượng khách thông 
qua các cảng hàng không trong 7T2026 đạt hơn 76 triệu lượt (+7% yoy), trong 
đó khách quốc tế đạt gần 30 triệu lượt (+11% yoy), trong khi khách nội địa ghi 
nhận tăng trưởng 4% yoy. Xu hướng này được hỗ trợ bởi nhu cầu du lịch quốc 
tế, chính sách thị thực thuận lợi và việc mở rộng mạng bay, trong khi tăng 
trưởng kinh tế và thu nhập tiếp tục tạo nền tảng cho nhu cầu đi lại trong nước. 
Trên cơ sở đó, chúng tôi dự phóng lượt khách quốc tế và nội địa tại các thị 
trường AST khai thác tăng bình quân lần lượt 11% và 5%/năm đến 2030, qua đó 
nâng dần tỷ trọng khách quốc tế trong tổng lưu lượng, thúc đẩy doanh 
thu/điểm kinh doanh nhờ: (1) lưu lượng hành khách qua cảng hàng không gia 
tăng và (2) tỷ trọng khách quốc tế cao hơn hỗ trợ doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi 
khách, đặc biệt tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

 Biểu đồ 7. Doanh thu/điểm kinh doanh AST (tỷ VND)

Nguồn: AST  Nguồn: AST

Mở rộng mạng lưới tại các nhà ga 
hiện hữu và sân bay mới, gia tăng 
quy mô kinh doanh đến 2030 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng chu kỳ mở rộng hạ tầng hàng không giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ 
tạo dư địa gia tăng quy mô kinh doanh cho AST, với số điểm kinh doanh dự 
phóng đạt 145 điểm vào cuối 2026 và tăng lên 182 điểm vào năm 2030. Cụ thể: 
- Trong ngắn hạn, tăng trưởng năm 2026 được hỗ trợ bởi việc ghi nhận 
đủ năm doanh thu từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất 
trong năm 2025, cùng 6 điểm mới tại T2 Nội Bài trong năm 2026, trong 
đó có 2 cửa hàng miễn thuế và 2 cửa hàng Memories of Vietnam khai 
trương trong 2Q2026. 
- Trong trung và dài hạn, các dự án Cảng HKQT Long Thành, Cảng HKQT 
Phú Quốc nâng cấp mở rộng và Cảng HKQT Gia Bình sẽ góp phần thúc 
đẩy ngành hàng không Việt Nam nói chung, bổ sung mặt bằng thương 
mại, mở ra cơ hội phát triển các mảng kinh doanh cốt lõi cho AST nói 
riêng bao gồm các mảng bán lẻ, miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood và 
phòng chờ thương gia. Với kinh nghiệm khai thác 141 điểm kinh doanh 
tại 6 sân bay quốc tế và lịch sử trúng thầu tại T3 Tân Sơn Nhất, T2 Nội 
Bài trong thời gian qua, chúng tôi kỳ vọng AST có khả năng cạnh tranh 
cao trong việc đấu thầu quyền khai thác mặt bằng tại các sân bay mới, 
đặc biệt là Gia Bình, qua đó duy trì vị thế và thị phần kinh doanh phi 
hàng không tại khu vực phía Bắc khi một phần lưu lượng hành khách 
được phân bổ lại từ Nội Bài.

Cơ cấu kinh doanh và hiệu quả vận 
hành hỗ trợ mở rộng biên lợi nhuận  
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng biên lợi nhuận của AST tiếp tục cải thiện trong 2026 - 2027, 
nhờ định hướng mở rộng các mảng có biên lợi nhuận tốt như hàng miễn thuế 
và phòng chờ thương gia, kết hợp tối ưu hiệu quả vận hành tại các điểm kinh 
doanh. Cụ thể: (1) AST ưu tiên gia tăng số điểm kinh doanh hàng miễn thuế 
nhằm đón đầu tăng trưởng nhanh và ổn định của lượng khách quốc tế, qua đó 
nâng đóng góp lợi nhuận từ hoạt động mua sắm tại sân bay, (2) mở rộng mạng 
lưới phòng chờ, đồng thời gia tăng lượng khách sử dụng trên nền năng lực phục 
vụ hiện hữu và (3) tối ưu hóa hiệu quả hoạt động thông qua cải thiện bố trí mặt 
bằng, danh mục sản phẩm và kiểm soát chi phí tại các điểm kinh doanh, qua 
đó tăng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thu được trên mỗi khách hàng.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá 
mục tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu AST, với giá mục tiêu 76,500 
VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 
21/09/2026, dựa trên triển vọng tăng trưởng từ lượng khách quốc tế, mở rộng 
mạng lưới điểm kinh doanh và khả năng cải thiện biên lợi nhuận của doanh 
nghiệp. Chúng tôi sử dụng phương pháp định giá từng phần (SOTP), bao gồm 
DCF cho hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi của AST và P/E mục tiêu 11x đối với phần 
lợi nhuận thuộc sở hữu AST tại VinaCS. Các giả định chính gồm: (1) lượng khách 
quốc tế/nội địa tăng trung bình 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030, (2) số điểm kinh 
doanh tăng từ 145 điểm cuối 2026 lên 182 điểm năm 2030, với giả định AST 
trúng thầu mặt bằng tại cả Long Thành và Gia Bình và (3) biên lợi nhuận gộp 
2026/2027 đạt 64.7/65.5%. Đối với VinaCS, chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhà máy suất ăn 
Long Thành sẽ hỗ trợ tăng trưởng lợi nhuận trong trung và dài hạn khi dự kiến 
có thể bắt đầu đóng góp lợi nhuận cho AST từ 2028.

Rủi ro đối với định giá bao gồm: (1) AST không giành được quyền khai thác tại 
Long Thành và Gia Bình như kỳ vọng, (2) lưu lượng khách dịch chuyển giữa các 
sân bay mới và hiện hữu khiến doanh thu tại một số điểm kinh doanh bị sụt 
giảm, (3) cạnh tranh gia tăng từ các doanh nghiệp kinh doanh dịch vụ phi hàng 
không khác và (4) căng thẳng địa chính trị kéo dài, giá dầu tăng gây áp lực lên 
hoạt động hàng không và chi tiêu của hành khách.

Source PDF: all report pdf/DDR5放量推动盈利增长，澜起科技Q3净利润同比预增153.6%~174.7%｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3

### Page 1

澜起科技 2026 年三季度预计营收约 20.79 亿元、同⽐增⻓约 46% ，归⺟净利润 12 亿⾄ 13 亿
元、同⽐增⻓超 150% ，盈利增速显著⾼于收⼊增速。增⻓主要受益于互连类芯⽚出货增加
及 DDR5 ⾼代际 RCD 芯⽚占⽐提升，⽑利率升⾄ 68% 以上。公司预计四季度相关产品需求
进⼀步提升，业绩仍有望延续增⻓。
摘要：
澜起科技三季度营收和利润均实现较快增⻓，盈利增速显著⾼于收⼊增速。公司预计， AI 推理及
Agent 应⽤发展带动服务器和内存需求增⻓， DDR5 ⾼代际产品出货占⽐提升，推动业绩进⼀步
改善。
10 ⽉ 20 ⽇，澜起科技披露 2026 年第三季度业绩预增公告，预计三季度实现营业收⼊约 20.79 亿
元，同⽐增⻓约 46.0% ，环⽐增⻓约 10.9% 。
利润⽅⾯，公司预计三季度归⺟净利润为 12 亿⾄ 13 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 153.6% ⾄ 174.7% ；扣⾮归
⺟净利润为 9.5 亿⾄ 10.5 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 152.6% ⾄ 179.2% ，环⽐增⻓ 32.2% ⾄ 46.1% 。

业绩增⻓主要受益于互连类芯⽚出货增加及产品结构改善。公司同时预计，第四季度相关产品
需求将进⼀步提升，后续业绩表现仍需关注需求兑现情况。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
DDR5 ⾼代际产品出货增⻓，⽑利率稳步提升
澜起科技预计，三季度互连类芯⽚产品线销售收⼊约 20.47 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 49.3% ，环⽐增⻓
20.9% ，占公司营业收⼊约 98.5% ，是本季度收⼊增⻓的主要来源。
产品结构⽅⾯， DDR5 第三、第四、第五⼦代 RCD （寄存器时钟驱动器）芯⽚出货占⽐进⼀步提
升。随着内存接⼝芯⽚向更⾼代际迭代，产品结构优化也为⽑利率改善提供了⽀撑。
公司预计，三季度互连类芯⽚产品线⽑利率为 68.0% ⾄ 68.5% ，同⽐提升 2.31 ⾄ 2.81 个百分点，
环⽐提升 0.57 ⾄ 1.07 个百分点。
⽑利率持续上⾏，意味着本季度利润增⻓不仅来⾃出货量增加，也受益于产品结构改善。营收
同⽐增⻓约 46% ，⽽归⺟净利润同⽐增⻓超过 150% ，反映出收⼊增⻓与利润率提升共同放⼤了

盈利弹性。
AI 推理需求带动内存互连芯⽚增⻓，四季度需求值得关注
随着 AI 推理及 Agent 应⽤发展，服务器对内存容量和带宽的需求持续增加， DDR5 内存及配套互
连芯⽚有望继续受益。澜起科技作为内存接⼝芯⽚供应商，主要通过相关芯⽚出货增⻓及产品
迭代获得增量。
对于后续需求，公司在公告中预计，第四季度相关产品需求将进⼀步提升。这为短期业绩延续
增⻓提供了⽀撑，但实际表现仍取决于服务器需求、产品出货及产品结构变化。
若需求按预期增⻓，叠加⾼代际产品占⽐继续提升，澜起科技的收⼊和利润仍有进⼀步增⻓的
空间。

Document 318: GF - PTFE Adoption

Source PDF: all report pdf/GF - PTFE Adoption.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Overseas Technology｜Oct 15,2026 
PTFE Adoption: 
Reshape the High-End Materials Landscape

Overseas  
Technology

Michelle Jing 
SFC CE No. BUK594 
michellejing@gfgroup.com.hk 
Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk

What's new 
Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE board prototype for the first time at OCP Global Summit 2026, highlighting seven supply-chain partners: 
Taiflex (8039 TT), Flexium (6269 TT), TTM Technologies (TTMI US), 3 leading Chinese PCB manufacturers, and 1 leading Chinese CCL 
supplier. We believe this development reinforces our previous view that PTFE remains the most promising material solution for the VR300 
NVL576 switch board, offering the best balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
Comments 
PTFE hybrid remains the preferred solution: As highlighted in our Oct. 5 report, we continue to favor the PTFE hybrid solution for the 
VR300 NVL576 switch board among the various options, given its optimal balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
While pure PTFE remains challenging to process, particularly in lamination and drilling, leading PCB manufacturers have achie ved 
encouraging yields with hybrid structures that combine PTFE signal layers with M9 materials in non-signal layers. 
We like PTFE's long-term adoption potential: PTFE applications in high-frequency and high-speed interconnects could extend beyond 
switch boards. The IEC has published a standard on PTFE-filled CCL, led by the leading Chinese CCL company, highlighting the material's 
relevance to broader circuit-board applications. Our checks also suggest that PTFE materials have entered the testing phase for other 
applications, including compute boards. We therefore remain positive on PTFE's long-term adoption across a broader range of high-end 
PCB applications. 
Limited near-term impact on conventional resin materials, but long-term substitution risks warrant attention: Under the current 
PTFE hybrid design, we expect 8–12 out of 32 layers to adopt PTFE for signal transmission, accounting for less than 50% of the total CCL 
resin content value. Over the longer term, we expect a potential shift toward pure PTFE solutions as improvements in processing 
technology enable commercially viable MP yields, reducing the need for hybrid structures. This could gradually increase substitution risks 
for conventional high-end PCB resin materials, including PPO and HC resin. 
Favor PTFE-exposed suppliers with proven technical and manufacturing capabilities: We remain positive on PTFE-related 
opportunities, particularly suppliers with strong R&D capabilities, proven MP expertise, and established customer qualifications. In CCL, 
we favor Taiflex (8039 TT) and leading Chinese CCL supplier for their PTFE material capabilities and supply-chain exposure. Among the 
five Nvidia-announced PCB suppliers, we favor the leading Chinese PTFE PCB supplier, given its advanced hybrid PTFE processing 
capabilities and encouraging yields. For CCL suppliers that have yet to enter the PTFE supply chain, we recommend monitoring progress 
in developing, while remaining alert to potential market-share losses in their conventional product portfolios. 
Risks  
1) AI demand slowing down; 2) Geo-political risks; 3) Competition.

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026

Source PDF: all report pdf/OPECプラス、２ヵ月連続で生産目標据え置き ～Ｇ７は備蓄放出の方針も、原油市場は生産枠より中東の供給制約を注視する展開が続く～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-05

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 10 月 5 日

1/3 
OPEC プラス、２ヵ月連続で生産目標据え置き 
～Ｇ７は備蓄放出の方針も、原油市場は生産枠より中東の供給制約を注視する展開が続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国は10 月４日の会合で、11 月の原油生産目標を９月の水準に据え置
くことを決定し、２ヵ月連続で増産を見送った。有志国は今年４月以降、2023 年に表明した
自主減産を段階的に縮小し、９月にはその解消を完了したものの、その後は市場環境や加盟
国の生産状況を見極める姿勢に転じている。 
○ ただし、今回の据え置きは原油高を容認したものではなく、イラン情勢の混乱によって中東
産油国が生産目標を引き上げても実際の供給を十分に増やせないことが背景にある。有志７
ヵ国の８月の生産量は、イラン情勢悪化前の２月を日量約500 万バレル下回り、湾岸産油国
の輸出も平時の６～８割程度にとどまる。このため、これまでの増産措置は供給不足の緩和
につながらず、国際原油価格は高止まりしている。Ｇ７は原油高対策を目的に、備蓄放出に
動く方針を打ち出したが、需給逼迫の解消には中東からの供給回復が不可欠である。 
○ また、OPEC プラスの合同閣僚監視委員会は、 海上輸送ルートの確保やエネルギー関連インフ
ラへの攻撃に強い懸念を示しており、原油市場の焦点は生産枠そのものより、ホルムズ海峡
を中心とする輸送ルートや中東産油国の生産 ・ 輸出能力に移っている。 先行きもOPEC プラス
が早期に増産路線へ復帰する可能性は低く、当面の原油価格はホルムズ海峡の航行状況や中
東の供給正常化に大きく左右される展開が続くと予想される。

主要産油国の枠組みである OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジアラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウ
ェート、 カザフスタン、 アルジェリア、 オマーン） は、10 月４日にオンラインで閣僚会合を開催し、
11 月の原油生産目標を９月の水準に据え置くことを決定した。９月６日に開催 した前回会合でも
10 月の生産目標を９月の水準に据え置いており（注１）、増産を見送るのは２ヵ月連続となる。有
志国は今後も毎月会合を開催して市場環境を点検し、次回会合は11 月１日に予定されている。 
有志国は、2023 年４月に表明した日量約165 万バレル規模の自主減産について、今年４月以降、

注１ ９月７日付レポート「OPEC 有志７ヵ国､自主減産終了後の10 月は様子見姿勢で合意」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/654829.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
段階的な縮小に動いてきた。特に７～９月は毎月日量 18.8 万バレルずつ生産目標を引き上げ、９
月には同自主減産の解消を完了した。一方、９月の会合では 10 月の生産目標を据え置く方針に転
じ、今回もその方針が維持された。 
OPEC プラスは、 世界的な景気や原油需要の動向など市場環境を見極めるとともに、 加盟国による
生産目標の順守状況にも目配りする姿勢を示している。もっとも、 今回の据え置き決定は原油高を
容認したと判断するのは適切ではないであろう。背景には、イラン情勢を巡る混乱により、中東産
油国が生産目標を引き上げても、 原油供給を十分に増やすことが難しい状況にあることが挙げられ
る。有志７ヵ国の８月の原油生産量は日量約 2,500 万バレルと、前月から 63 万バレル増加したも
のの、 イラン情勢が悪化する前の２月と比較して約 500 万バレル少ない水準にとどまった。 湾岸産
油国の原油輸出も、このところ平時の６～８割程度にとどまる状況が続いている。このため、これ
までの生産目標引き上げの多くは「絵に描いた餅」となり、原油市場の供給不足を十分に緩和する
には至っていない。 
こうした状況を反映して、 国際原油価格は高止まりしている（図１）。このため、原油高によるイ
ンフレ圧力や世界経済への悪影響が懸
念される。こうしたなか、Ｇ７（主要７
ヵ国）は９月２日、ＩＥＡ（国際エネル
ギー機関） を通じて原油や石油製品など
合計１億バレルを４ヵ月にわたって協
調放出する方針を表明した。 特に、供給
逼迫が深刻な軽油について、当初 20 日
間に前倒しで放出することで市場の安
定を図るとしている。 しかし、 有志７ヵ
国の生産量が２月と比較して日量約500
万バレル少ないことを勘案すれば、 備蓄
放出のみで需給逼迫を解消することは難しく、中東地域からの供給回復が不可欠である。 
さらに注目されるのが、 有志国会合と同日に開催されたOPEC プラスの合同閣僚監視委員会 （JMMC）
の声明である。JMMC は「国際的な海上輸送ルートを確保し、 エネルギーの流れを途絶させないこと
が極めて重要」との認識を示した。また、エネルギー関連インフラへの攻撃についても「損傷した
設備の復旧には多額の費用と時間を要し、 供給能力そのものに悪影響を与える」との懸念を示した。
これは、足元の原油市場における最大の問題が OPEC プラスの生産目標ではなく、ホルムズ海峡を
中心とする輸送ルートや中東産油国の生産・輸出能力に移っていることを示唆する。 
先行きについても、OPEC プラスが早期に増産路線に復帰する可能性は低いとみられる。OPEC プ
ラス全体では、なお日量約200 万バレル規模の減産措置が年末まで残されている。一方、加盟国の
実際の生産能力を精査する作業は、 イラン情勢に伴う混乱により遅れている模様である。生産能力
の評価は、2027 年以降の各国の生産基準を決定するうえで重要となるため、OPEC プラスとしても
目先の追加増産より、2027 年以降の生産体制の構築に軸足を移している可能性が高い。

3/3
このため、 当面の原油価格を左右する材料としては、OPEC プラスが生産枠をどの程度引き上げる
か以上に、ホルムズ海峡の航行状況や中東産油国の輸出回復が重要になる。有志国は 11 月１日に
12 月の生産方針を協議する予定であり、その後、11 月 29 日には JMMC も開催される。中東情勢の
先行きが見通しにくいなか、OPEC プラスにとっては、 原油価格の安定と世界市場への供給確保を両
立させる難しい政策運営が続くことになろう。
